# Desktop to Web Migration Status

# Last reviewed: 2026-10-18

## Scope and evidence

The migration target is the web-compatible UI and workflows in the AYEC Pro desktop application. This record tracks verified slices and open work; a passing focused test is not proof of whole-application parity.

The current static inventory command is `python Web_Arayuzu/tools/desktop_web_gap_audit.py`. Its latest run reports 43 desktop page names and 43 matching web labels, 114 generated dialog catalog entries, 178 desktop setting keys, no unexplained missing setting keys, and 1,401 desktop Qt signal connection calls. The four excluded setting keys are the three native printer preferences and the host-specific `voice_input_device`; the web provides browser-scoped microphone selection and testing instead. These are inventory counts, not parity proofs. A local Ollama qwen3:4b classification request for the 50 KB desktop contracts page timed out after 180 seconds; its follow-up used a narrow rg-first source scan. A second focused qwen3:4b classification request for the global-search snippets timed out after 120 seconds; the follow-up scanned only the named search class, handler, navigation routes, and result logic. For the loan-payment slice, qwen2.5-coder:7b classified the Python dialog as below 80% and the web mapping as above 80%; its rationale reflected programming-language stereotypes instead of defect evidence, so the label was treated only as triage and the specific loan route and calculation symbols were checked directly. The stock-locations route retains its detailed page heading and uses the desktop navigation label.

Fresh inventory on 2026-10-15 reports 43/43 desktop page labels with none missing, a web dialog catalog, 178 desktop setting keys and 161 web keys with no missing keys, and the three native-only printer settings (`print_document_printer`, `print_label_printer`, `print_receipt_printer`). The inventory also records 334 web dialog signal calls and 1,401 desktop Qt signal connections. These counts do not verify every workflow or visual state. A focused `rg` scan found no remaining `window.confirm`, `window.prompt`, or `window.alert` calls in `Web_Arayuzu/web`; printer selection and direct printer access remain native-only.

## Native-only exclusions and open gaps

- Native-only: printer device selection and direct printer access. The three printer preference keys (`print_document_printer`, `print_label_printer`, `print_receipt_printer`) remain desktop-owned; web printing uses the browser print flow and cannot select a host printer through the browser API.
- Native-only: the desktop `voice_input_device` value stores a host microphone index for the Qt speech listener. Browser device IDs belong to the browser profile and are saved separately as `ayec.web.voice.input-device`; the web voice page enumerates devices after browser permission and tests the selected device through `getUserMedia`. The browser cannot enumerate or configure the host Qt microphone index, so the desktop key is classified as native-only rather than a missing web setting. Hidden Edge verified device enumeration, selected-device capture, and microphone stream/audio-context cleanup in the desktop-header audit.
- Native-only: reconciliation of the desktop offline synchronization queue. The web UI uses authenticated server APIs and does not own the desktop process queue.
- Native-only: minimize, maximize, close, and taskbar/window-focus controls belong to the Qt application window. Web_Arayuzu runs inside the host browser and does not control the host window; the unmatched PC Builder toolbar controls are recorded in the matched visual follow-up.
- Open licensing capability: the legacy `LicenseKeygenDialog` derives an HWID-bound key with the desktop `SecurityManager`; do not port that local algorithm. Its web dispatcher now routes to the central license flow. The central API creates orders scoped by tenant, product, hardware ID, and installation ID; administrator approval requires a reported payment and generates the license code centrally. There is no direct administrator grant endpoint for an arbitrary HWID, so that legacy behavior remains open pending an approved central issuance policy.
- Customer 360 admin user action: the desktop `Kullan\u0131c\u0131 Ekle` button navigates to the Companies list; it does not create a central-operator user. The web action now reproduces that navigation inside the combined tenant list/detail screen by bringing the selected company into view and focusing the list search. Creating users remains in tenant-admin user management; the central API has no create-user endpoint.
- Open provider behavior: live Nominatim search and reverse-geocoding returned results for Balikesir coordinates through the desktop-compatible backend functions. A forced Nominatim failure also exercised the live Photon fallback successfully. The customer address UI is covered with authenticated endpoint mocks; Tenant administrators can store a DPAPI-protected TomTom key through the web map; live TomTom tiles and credential-backed routing remain unverified until a valid tenant key is supplied. Hidden Edge verifies both the missing-key dashed draft fallback and a mocked successful route response that replaces the draft with returned geometry, displays 4.2 km, and exposes sharing. The offline location-picker callback is covered. The focused field-service backend suite passes 6 tests for geocoding, route normalization, key handling, vector and traffic proxies, and tenant scoping; it does not exercise a live TomTom map session. Latest 43-route desktop/mobile sweep and route-response fixture: `output/playwright/web-route-smoke-route-success-20261018/`.
- Proforma template editor parity: hidden Edge verifies settings navigation, formatting and token insertion, paste sanitization, successful activation, rejected PDF generation preserving the inactive state, authenticated tenant PDF upload/removal, base64 omission, the legacy-path notice, mobile layout, oversized-PDF rejection, invalid-PDF rejection, cleared file input, and unchanged tenant state after rejected files. The authenticated roundtrip uses the real tenant API for writes; paste and formatting checks still use fixtures for unrelated editor actions; the authenticated browser follow-up below exercises HTML activation and PDF generation through the tenant API. The bootstrap response reports only whether a legacy local PDF path exists, never the path itself; using that old file in the web editor requires selecting and uploading it again. Evidence: `output/playwright/pc-builder-visual-audit-result-20261018.json`, `tools/quality/proforma_template_authenticated_roundtrip.cjs`, and the focused test `tests/test_web_pc_builder_persistence_api.py::test_pc_builder_quote_note_and_finance_persist_in_authenticated_tenant`.
- Open parity: all page actions and dialog states are not yet proven end-to-end. Matched captures still show residual sidebar, PC Builder, appointment-calendar, receipt glyph rasterization, and remaining job-report row raster differences; job-report customer-name, table-font, and row-origin alignment lowered the matched 996x282 crop RGB MAE from 9.988 to 8.546 (86.14% exact pixels; p95 delta 59). The latest paired 1300x850 PC Builder comparison measures full-shell RGB MAE 7.740 with 70.07% exact pixels, page crop (x=260..1299,y=70..829) MAE 7.009 with 77.56% exact pixels, and visible-row crop (x=260..1299,y=310..824) MAE 6.797 with 78.73% exact pixels. The quantity-stepper crop MAE is 4.074; the 135x34 form-factor select crop (x=619..753,y=371..404) now measures RGB MAE 7.730 with 82.985% exact pixels, down from 15.967 and 73.203% before moving the selector 1px right and 2px up on the same 1300x850 capture, and the 148x34 price-stepper crop (x=1071..1218,y=368..401) now measures RGB MAE 9.344 with 76.47% exact pixels, down from 12.454 and 46.363% before alignment. The nine-column web grid uses the native 1172px table width at 1300px inside a synchronized horizontal scroll viewport (989px visible, 183px scroll range); all fields including total, stock, and remove are reachable, and the table fits at 1440px. Pixel parity remains open. Hidden Edge has 1300x850 Koyu Mavi baselines for all 44 web route targets; 42 of 43 desktop page IDs have routed-page references; the technician panel is captured separately as a dialog. Paired pixel review is partial and open. The route harness suppresses the temporary server first-run wizard and stubs direct finance-ledger reads so the public landing screen cannot leak into route captures. The latest sweep at `output/playwright/pc-builder-quantity-stepper-only-20261018/` confirms 43 desktop-mapped page IDs plus the web Control Center, 44 desktop and mobile route targets, matching route state and body page markers, mapped AI Assistant page ID 170, verified two-tab Support Center actions, hidden auth root, dashboard shortcut grids of three columns at 1300px, two at 1280px, and one on mobile, desktop job-report metrics of 6 jobs and 15 services, twelve report-row SVG actions, the two-row job-report header bounds, four fully visible report rows with summary cards still visible, and zero browser exceptions. The user-manual route loads the repository Markdown document in the offscreen fixture and asserts its title and rendered heading count instead of capturing a blank login-required response.

On matched 1300x850 captures, full-image RGB MAE is 6.787 and content-crop MAE is 6.026; the calendar region MAE is 4.894, with toolbar and KPI differences still visible. The 43 desktop routes, Control Center, and 44 mobile routes pass with zero browser exceptions. Focused appointments parity tests cover the active renderer and workflow.
- Quick-note settings: note rows now support desktop double-click editing and the right-click Edit/Delete menu. Both the context-menu Delete and inline trash action open a confirmation dialog before issuing the tenant-scoped delete write. Headless Chromium verified the edit dialog, menu actions, confirmation dialog, zero writes before confirmation, and the delete payload after confirmation; focused source checks verify the desktop bindings and web handlers.
- Vehicle history and QR: a per-card action now opens the desktop-equivalent maintenance and service history tables, shows the existing share token, copies the `ayec://vehicle-history/...` URI, and displays a locally generated QR image. Both history and QR endpoints require the active tenant session. Unit tests verify tenant-database use, PNG generation, auth responses, and the browser flow was exercised headlessly.
- Audit logs: the web page now has server-backed search, module, severity, and time filters, 25-row pagination, live refresh/pause, raw table-column toggle, page and selected-event CSV export, and a desktop-style selected-event inspector. Tenant admins can suspend another account from the inspector; the authenticated endpoint prevents self-suspension, enforces administrator role, scopes to the active tenant database, and records the action in `audit_logs`. The selected-event report uses the browser print-to-PDF flow and discloses that it contains recorded data without claiming cryptographic verification. Focused tests cover filters, pagination, suspension authorization, audit recording, tenant session routing, and self-suspension denial; headless browser checks exercised selection, page requests, filters, the report content, and the suspension request with no page errors.
- User management: administrators can create a tenant user with the desktop fields, create a linked personnel record in the same transaction, apply role-derived page permissions, store a validated password hash, and record `USER_CREATED` in the tenant audit log. The selected-user profile also supports the desktop delete action: it marks the account deleted/archived or inactive using the installed schema, blocks self-deletion, and writes `USER_DELETED` in the same tenant transaction. Both endpoints enforce administrator role and active-tenant scope; creation rejects duplicate usernames/emails and invalid fields and rolls back both records if audit insertion fails. Focused unit and authenticated HTTP tests verified tenant database use, delete fallbacks, rollback, and that list responses omit password hashes; headless settings-screen checks cover user creation and deletion with no page errors.
- User management context menu: right-clicking a user row now exposes the desktop Edit and Delete actions. Edit opens the existing permission editor; Delete uses the existing confirmation and tenant-scoped delete endpoint, including self-delete protection. A headless Chromium fixture opened the menu and invoked both handlers. Screenshot: `output/playwright/user-management-context-headless.png`.
- Field service personnel: the field map reads active field technicians and their stored GPS coordinates, role, status, and last-seen time from the active tenant. The Leaflet map displays every located technician at once, fits the viewport to the markers, and focuses the selected card or marker. Personnel cards support a finish-duty button and the desktop right-click finish-duty action. Finishing a duty confirms in the browser and updates the tenant personnel status to idle. Authenticated HTTP tests verify tenant scoping, inactive/deleted filtering, location data, and finish-duty persistence. Headless Chromium verified two simultaneous markers, map framing, focus selection, context menu, finish endpoint payload, and explicit browser GPS start/stop. Browser sharing requires an authenticated account linked to an active field personnel row and browser location permission; updates target only that account's linked record. Screenshot: `output/playwright/field-service-multi-marker-headless.png`; GPS screenshot: `output/playwright/field-service-browser-location-headless.png`. The web 3D vector toggle, map state synchronization, and return to 2D are implemented; actual TomTom account calls remain unverified.
- Field-service route fallback UX: a missing TomTom key or unavailable route now produces a specific warning while keeping the drawn draft route visible. The focused field-service test checks both response reasons and warning dispatch; credential-backed live routing remains unverified.
- Field-service route, search, and traffic follow-up: added desktop nearest-stop ordering from the configured office center, TomTom road geometry and live-traffic duration, a server-proxied TomTom flow layer using the locally encrypted key, coordinate parsing, address result selection, and Nominatim-to-Photon geocoding fallback through authenticated endpoints. Unit tests verify provider fallback, geometry normalization, tile validation, authentication, and that the key is not returned to the browser. Headless Chromium fixtures verified two technicians, route geometry/distance/time, multi-result address selection, traffic toggle, and mobile layout without horizontal overflow; console errors/warnings: zero. Screenshots: `output/playwright/field-service-tomtom-route-headless.png`, `output/playwright/field-service-traffic-headless.png`, and `output/playwright/field-service-traffic-mobile-headless.png`. The web now builds the desktop TomTom vector-map bundle as a separate static asset, uses the SDK proxy-credentials mode, and routes same-origin authenticated vector resource requests through the stored server key. Offscreen Chromium with a local mock style, sprite, and empty vector tiles reached map-ready status after 13 same-origin vector resource requests; no direct TomTom requests or API-key values appeared in browser URLs. The mock has no production vector layers, so SDK style warnings remain expected. A separate UI fixture verified 2D/3D toggling, personnel/route/traffic state forwarding, and route preservation on return. Actual TomTom account calls and exact desktop pixel matching remain unverified. Screenshots: `output/playwright/field-service-vector-toggle-headless.png` and `output/playwright/field-service-vector-map-mock-headless.png`.
- Knowledge base: the web page mirrors the desktop article list and read-only detail split, title/tag search, add form, selected-article deletion, copy-content action, and right-click menu. The menu follows desktop order and emoji labels; its actions now target the article that was right-clicked. Deletion uses a shared 350x180 confirmation dialog with Hayir/Evet actions, and cancel sends no write request. Headless Chromium verified menu order, copying the second article while the first was selected, cancel => zero writes, confirm => delete ID 2, dialog size/actions, and zero console errors. Screenshot: `output/playwright/knowledge-base-delete-confirm-headless.png`. Focused HTTP tests verify tenant-scoped create/search/update/delete and anonymous denial. The automotive sector also shows its three desktop reference links. Pixel comparison against Qt remains open.
- External service tracking: the desktop `logistics` page reads and writes `external_warranty_tracking`; the earlier web view used the separate `logistics` table. The web page now has a desktop-aligned external service and warranty view with the seven status choices, record form, cargo numbers, notes, search, status/service/month filters, four-row pagination, optional multi-select, edit/delete, context actions, multi-select XLSX export, XLSX/XLS/CSV import, print, cargo-manifest preview, and a browser photo gallery with bounded tenant-local uploads. The existing simple cargo tracking view remains reachable in a separate tab and continues to use its own table. The web Document Center lists tenant logistics photos, stock photos, project contracts, and contract attachments without exposing local paths; it opens managed external-tracking photos and tenant contract attachments through authenticated URLs and marks desktop-local files unavailable. An authenticated active-tenant HTTP test verifies create/search/update/delete, photo upload/list/serve/delete, import/export, Document Center access, and anonymous denial. Headless Chromium fixtures exercised render, filters, tabs, add/edit/delete, multi-select export, imports, gallery CRUD, manifest generation, and Document Center list/refresh/open against mocked browser APIs. Existing desktop-local photo, stock-photo, and project-contract paths require migration or re-upload; no pixel match is claimed.
- Service status: double-clicking a web table row opens the desktop-equivalent detail dialog, and row actions now include browser printing and SMS composition. The SMS dialog has the desktop templates, tracking-number substitution, phone/recipient details, a live character count, and send/disabled states. The SMS settings page now includes Twilio account credentials and a connection check; the auth token is encrypted at rest and account SID/token values are omitted from settings and bootstrap responses. Tenant-scoped `/api/desktop/service-sms` sends only for a current, non-deleted tenant device, normalizes the phone number, caps messages at 1,600 characters, and logs the returned provider SID in the tenant SMS log. Focused tests mock the Twilio API and verify settings masking/encryption, authenticated tenant routing, connection check, request body/auth, message logging, and invalid inputs; headless Chromium exercised the actual dialog, template substitution, counter, and send payload. No real SMS was sent. This does not prove the full live page or desktop pixel match.
- Backups: tenant administrators can create a SQLite-consistent snapshot through the central `support_backups` registry and download only available archives belonging to their active tenant and product. The live `backup-reference.js` page lists archive metadata and SHA-256, creates snapshots, refreshes the list, and downloads a selected database archive. Company administrators can upload a desktop SQLite backup; integrity, tenant company name, and signed-in account identity are checked before it is staged as a central restore source. Admin_Konsol remains responsible for issuing the restore command. A server worker now checks each active tenant's daily preference every 30 seconds, applies its saved IANA timezone, catches up after the configured time, and records one successful run per local date through the same central integrity and audit path. Enabled `backup_on_exit` preferences create a tenant backup on explicit administrator logout. Focused tests cover integrity, audit recording, role and tenant/product isolation, metadata-bound download, timezone conversion and schedule idempotency. Headless checks verified the time input disables/enables with the daily switch and exercised a mocked archive download. Central restore remains in Admin Konsol.

Technician panel follow-up (2026-10-18): the technician form now provides native-equivalent General Overview, Test Application, and History tabs. The checklist reads the active tenant device-profile/sector  Evidence: output/playwright/technician-history-offscreen-20261018.json; full route sweep: output/playwright/technician-tabs-parity-20261018/runner-result.json.
ast_notes catalog with preset fallback, hides inactive items, and saves selections immediately through an authenticated endpoint that updates only devices.checklist_status. The lazy history tab combines native logs entries and non-deleted used-part entries; its authenticated API validates the active service and caps results at 500 events. Focused tests cover tenant isolation, profile catalog selection, inactive items, checklist-only writes, event ordering, and unknown-service rejection. Hidden Edge verified all three tabs, active-checklist persistence, retention of selected inactive entries, history rendering, and dynamic service-report tests with zero browser exceptions. Matched desktop tab geometry remains unverified.

The browser checks used sample data and did not prove every workflow against every tenant schema. Qt offscreen captures have shown font glyph issues in this environment, so they do not establish pixel-perfect desktop equivalence.

## Native-only exclusions

- Direct selection or enumeration of Windows printer queues is excluded from browser parity. The static inventory identifies `print_document_printer`, `print_label_printer`, and `print_receipt_printer` as native-only settings. Browser print output remains in scope through the browser print flow.
- The desktop `voice_input_device` stores a Qt host microphone index. The web uses permission-gated browser device IDs in browser-profile storage and exposes a microphone level test; the hidden Edge audit verified the selected browser input and stopped its stream and audio context after testing.
- Direct operating-system device, driver, and arbitrary local-path access is excluded where the browser sandbox prevents it. Use browser-supported uploads, downloads, print, or device APIs when they provide an equivalent workflow; those web-compatible paths remain in scope.
- No serial-port feature has been confirmed as native-only by the current focused source scan. Do not add it to the exclusion list without source evidence.

## Remaining gaps

- Fiscal controls: the web now shows a tenant database integrity/preflight summary, bank and customer opening counts, and TRY total. The rollover flow asks for the desktop accountant attestation, current user password, and per-category selections; it invokes the desktop FiscalYearService against a connection-only adapter bound to the active tenant database. Its snapshot is stored as a protected `fiscal-rollover` entry in the central AYEC backup registry, and completed rollover actions are audited. Fiscal archive selection reads finance rows from tenant-owned, checksum-verified snapshots in a paginated view with full CSV export. Users can activate an archive as the read-only database context across web pages; the selected archive travels with each API request, `active_db_path()` resolves to the checksum-verified snapshot, bootstrap skips live repair writes, and business POSTs are rejected. Returning to live clears the archive selection and browser data cache. Focused tests cover rollover gates, central snapshot uniqueness, archive integrity, tenant isolation, bootstrap from the archive, live/archive table separation, blocked writes, and live-data restoration after leaving archive mode. Headless browser checks covered the fiscal controls, archive activation, active request header, banner, and return-to-live action; a real HTTP integration test confirmed archive-backed bootstrap/table responses, write rejection, and live reads after context removal. Company administrators can import existing desktop `archive_YYYY.db` files from the tenant database directory or legacy database directory. Import verifies SQLite integrity, the active company name, and a matching signed-in account identity before creating a protected, checksum-verified central tenant backup; the web can then open it as a read-only fiscal context. Files outside those server-side directories and archives without verifiable account and company identity remain unavailable. Pixel comparison against a live desktop render remains unverified because a matching live desktop page render has not yet been captured.

- Audit the behavior of all 43 pages against their desktop page implementations, including error states, permissions, edit/delete flows, pagination, context menus, keyboard interactions, and tenant isolation. The latest hidden Edge route smoke confirms all 43 routes load at desktop and mobile widths without browser exceptions or horizontal overflow; it also exercises automotive stock adjustments, vehicle-maintenance details/history/QR, and a field-route draft. This is not page-by-page action coverage; the listed behavior cases remain open beyond the individually verified slices below.
- Sidebar visual parity: a matched full-shell pair captures the dashboard at 1280x800 with the service group expanded and dashboard selected. The native and web sidebar crops are both 256x800; RGB MAE is 9.693 with 58.13% exact pixels. Full-shell MAE is 11.194. The web brand logo, 88px brand card, selected-row accent, and child-row indent follow the native reference more closely; icon rasterization, typography, and spacing differences remain. The hidden Edge audit verifies the live tenant shell and 73 route targets. A separate authenticated Technician check now verifies role-based route visibility with permissions returned by the tenant session; broad route scenarios still use synthetic roles.
- Review all 114 current dialog catalog definitions and their business logic. The static audit finds 85 direct web routes and zero actionable unmapped workflows; the generated catalog and route count do not prove that every dialog workflow is implemented and verified end-to-end.
- Map the 1,401 desktop Qt signal connections to web events and verify behavior per feature. The presence of generic web context-menu, double-click, and responsive handlers is only indirect evidence.
- Run representative authenticated API checks for each migrated workflow and each supported schema family.
- Google Drive web backup: the page accepts a Google OAuth Web application client JSON, shows the exact HTTPS callback URI, starts an offline `drive.file` consent flow, stores client secrets and refresh tokens encrypted in the active tenant database, validates one-time CSRF state on the callback, and uploads a centrally integrity-checked backup to Drive. Focused tests cover client type validation, secret encryption, authorization parameters, state expiry/reuse, and mocked upload bytes; a headless browser check verified the connected status, enabled upload action, and callback display. Live Google consent and upload still require a tenant's registered OAuth client and were not claimed as verified.
- Remaining backup verification: live Google consent and upload still require an authorized tenant's registered OAuth Web client; the callback/exchange and Drive upload were verified only with mocked credentials. The Admin Konsol restore queue, tenant selection, download, confirmation, and mobile layout have now passed the hidden-browser audit. The desktop provider uses a local OAuth callback and shared `token.pickle`, while the web flow uses a registered callback and tenant-scoped encrypted credentials. Closing a browser tab cannot reliably trigger the desktop process-exit backup; the web equivalent runs on explicit administrator logout.
- Check and note portfolio: row actions now match the desktop status gates, including the bank receipt detail and return action for endorsed records. The right-click status menu uses the shared row binder, which also supplies touch long-press and keyboard context triggers. Collection/payment asks for an optional bank account; the tenant-scoped status API commits the instrument update, portfolio accounting entry, and optional bank movement together, then records audit metadata. Focused tests cover desktop finance-side-effect mapping, bank-account validation, rollback, and tenant isolation; an offscreen browser check verified the context menu, endorsed-row actions, account picker, and status request payload. The create form now mirrors the desktop fields and validates amount, date, and issuer/recipient before using a tenant-scoped create endpoint; it also writes the desktop initial finance entry and audit event. Generic inserts use the same path, and generic edits cannot bypass the dedicated status workflow. Detail actions open a read-only instrument summary. Focused tests cover create payload sanitization, initial accounting data, and invalid form data.
- Bank account transfers: the web dialog now defaults its source to the selected account, excludes that source from targets, includes inactive-aware account labels and live source balance, and caps the amount at the available balance. Headless verification changed source accounts, checked the distinct-target list and balance limit, then captured the transfer payload. Tenant-scoped API helper tests cover active-account validation, amount bounds, description limits, failure cleanup, and actor tenant isolation. Transfer and generic bank-account write operations now require an admin role; offscreen checks verified disabled management buttons for Manager and enabled buttons for Admin, while focused API tests verify unauthorized transfers stop before opening the tenant database. Bank cards now expose a desktop-style context menu; administrators can view details, edit, transfer, deactivate, or delete, while managers see read-only detail. Account detail includes desktop metadata and the latest 50 finance movements. Headless checks verified Admin and Manager context actions, metadata, movement history, and the single visible close action.
- Bank statement preview: the desktop CSV export action is now available in the web dialog footer, downloads every parsed OFX/MT940 row with UTF-8 BOM, and keeps the preview open. The broken digit and line regexes in the browser parser were corrected; headless checks parsed an MT940 transaction and exported all 301 OFX rows while displaying the first 300, with the correct filename, headers, and close/export actions.
- Contract attachments: the contracts grid has an attachments action and desktop-style row double-click details with contract metadata, multi-file upload, download/open, and confirmed deletion. The tenant-scoped API stores files separately per tenant, sanitizes names, limits each file to 25 MB, and migrates legacy desktop files from the application's direct `contract_docs` folder into tenant storage when first viewed or downloaded. Focused tests cover authenticated upload/download/delete, rejected input, and legacy migration; a headless Chromium fixture exercised the detail dialog, multiple-file input flow, and API payloads. A second hidden Edge run signed in as a tenant-local Admin, loaded real contract details, created and deleted an attachment through the active tenant API, and verified the tenant database and isolated file storage; browser exceptions: 0. Pixel comparison remains open.
- Personnel daily-wage workflow: the personnel page now has the desktop's Puantaj and Izinler tab, period/person selector, work-day input, live wage calculation, and period table with edit/delete. The tenant-scoped endpoint creates or updates one monthly record and its accounting expense together, and deletion removes both in one transaction; administrator-only mutations are checked in the API as well as the interface. Focused HTTP tests verify tenant login, record upsert, accounting side effects, deletion, invalid ranges, and anonymous denial. Headless Chromium exercised the tab, calculation, save, list refresh, and delete against the actual browser code with a local API mock. Broader attendance/leave tracking is not present in the inspected desktop tab, and matched-size desktop pixel comparison remains unverified.
- Vehicle-maintenance photo gallery is implemented with the desktop labels, carousel, browser uploads, authenticated tenant-scoped serving/deletion, and bounded image uploads. Legacy absolute photo paths under the known application `assets/maintenance_photos` folder now migrate lazily into the active tenant's storage after path, image signature, and size checks; original desktop files remain untouched. Paths outside that folder or unsupported/corrupt/oversize files stay unavailable. A hidden Edge run loaded a valid legacy PNG through the tenant API, displayed it in the actual gallery, and verified the tenant DB path update and tenant-local file copy.
- Complete matched-size desktop/web visual comparisons. Current Qt offscreen font rendering is an environment limitation that must be resolved before claiming pixel parity.
- Re-run the static inventory after each migration slice and update this status with newly verified coverage and any source-supported native-only exclusions.

Headless Edge rendered the actual dialog builder, changed a custom value, and verified the submit callback sends that value, integer order and active state, title, content, ID, and update action to `fast_notes`; screenshot: `output/playwright/quick-note-editor-parity-headless.png`. Fresh-schema and legacy-file tests confirm these two columns are created, older note rows survive the upgrade, and both fields round-trip. An authenticated HTTP test creates an isolated tenant, inserts a fast note, reads it back, updates its custom category/title/content/order/active state, and verifies the persisted values. The HTTP test uses a temporary registry and tenant database; focused schema, API, desktop reference, and web parity tests pass (6). - Stock context menu: added a tenant-authenticated stock-photo response that accepts a part ID, limits MIME types and file size, and never returns the stored path. The menu opens the photo preview, invokes browser print/PDF, and downloads an authenticated XLSX workbook. Deletion accepts the signed-in user's password, the configured emergency master key, or the tenant administrator password, matching the desktop confirmation flow. Incorrect credentials are rejected before stock changes; successful deletion records a stock movement and accounting reversal. Manual stock adjustments use a tenant-authenticated endpoint that writes the movement, updates stock locations, and records desktop-equivalent purchase or shrinkage expenses in one database transaction. The workbook exports the desktop field order, filters deleted cards, and calculates currency rate, TRY purchase and sale prices, and original and TRY stock values. Focused tests cover both adjustment directions, overdraw rejection, finance metadata, route authentication, all three accepted password sources, photo access, and USD workbook calculations. Headless Chromium verified the adjustment dialog posts a signed delta and reference to the endpoint. Screenshot: output/playwright/stock-context-menu-headless.png.

The table separates delivered date from estimated target date; the target-date checkbox defaults off to match the desktop. Appointment double-click ID parity (2026-10-16): Ollama qwen2.5-coder:7b classified the bounded desktop/web appointment excerpts as Risk/Hata Yok (95%, 90%, 85%), but direct type-flow review found both web double-click handlers compared the database ID strictly to `dataset.id`, a string. Desktop emits integer appointment IDs from `SidePanelCard`; SQLite-backed API records can also carry numeric IDs, so double-click could resolve no appointment and fail to open edit. Both handlers now compare normalized strings. Regression coverage: 12 appointment parity tests pass, JavaScript syntax and source guards pass, and the appointment dialog's hidden Edge palette audit reports zero browser exceptions. The generic double-click data-resolution path has source-level regression coverage; a dedicated browser event dispatch check remains open.
Appointment numeric-ID double-click event verification (2026-10-16): extended the hidden Edge appointment audit to render a real side-panel card with numeric database id 93, dispatch a bubbling `dblclick` event, and assert that the edit dialog opens with the expected customer while the DOM dataset id is a string. The run confirms numeric id/string dataset resolution and zero browser exceptions. The dedicated browser event gap noted above is closed for the appointment side-panel path.
Appointment double-click audit strengthening (2026-10-16): the hidden Edge fixture now switches the live application route to appointments and calls the actual `render()` path before dispatching the bubbling double-click on the rendered side-panel card. It confirms the edit title/customer for numeric model id versus string DOM id with 0 browser exceptions; this replaces the earlier direct-render-only fixture setup.

Responsive desktop sidebar width (2026-10-17): the web shell now scales its expanded sidebar from 240 to 320 px at 20 vw, matching the desktop `SideMenu.expanded_width_for_window` rule. Service invoice workflow migration (2026-10-17): added an authenticated tenant-scoped API for uninvoiced TRY and foreign-currency accounting rows, transactional source-specific marking, and PDF generation through the desktop invoice templates. Overall migration and interaction audit remain open.

Desktop shell geometry correction (2026-10-17): the Qt main window places its 70px header across the full 1300px client area, then places the sidebar and content below it, with a 16px status strip at the bottom. The web shell had placed the header only over the content column and let its document grow to 870px, creating a vertical scrollbar and 15px width loss. Updated the desktop layout to a full-width header row, sidebar/content second row, and fixed viewport area above the status strip. Hidden Edge confirms 1300 x 850 viewport/document size, header at [0,0,1300,70], sidebar at [0,70,260,764], and main viewport at [260,70,1040,764], with zero browser exceptions. The new internal main scroller also resets to the top on page navigation. The paired full-shell RGB MAE improved from 17.109 to 16.123; sidebar MAE is 14.642, so visual parity remains open. The appointment functional capture passes and the complete web-selected suite passes (617, 946 deselected, one dependency deprecation warning). The desktop shell capture and headless capture are under artifacts/desktop_shell_reference/ and output/playwright/.

Desktop header navigation slice (2026-10-17): added the desktop app icon/name and a disabled-until-available Back button at the Qt reference positions (Back [8,19,80,32], icon [92,30,10,10], app name [106,25,64,19], active page [174,17,73,36]). Back navigation now follows the web page stack and page changes reset the internal main viewport. The headless appointment fixture uses the normal navigation function so its active title matches the desktop; hidden Edge verifies Back returns to the previous page and restores forward navigation with zero exceptions. Corrected CSS so the `hidden` Management Center button honors its hidden attribute. Focused appointment/navigation tests pass (35), and the complete web-selected suite passes (617, 946 deselected, one dependency deprecation warning). The full-shell paired RGB MAE is 15.797, topbar MAE 20.82, and sidebar MAE 13.745. Header/content visual mismatch and broader page/dialog audits remain open.

Desktop header actions slice (2026-10-17): aligned the remaining web header controls with the 1300 x 850 Qt reference: assistant, sync, notifications, settings, theme picker, and profile now occupy the measured desktop boxes. Sector route smoke audit (2026-10-17): headless Playwright visited all 86 route/sector combinations exposed for Technical Service and Automotive, excluding the restricted Management Center. ## 2026-10-17 Bulk loan payment mapping

- Replaced the generic customer payment picker mapping for bulk_payment_dialog with a loan installment workflow.
- The web flow now supports selecting installments, paying selected or all installments, and early closure with the desktop calculation summary.
- Verified dialog dispatch tests, the headless offscreen interaction audit, and the web test selection: 634 passed.
- Full dialog parity remains in progress; the desktop manifest still contains unmigrated generic dialogs and workflows.

Bulk loan payment parity follow-up (2026-10-17): matched the desktop initial unpaid amount summary and early-closure penalty control (0-5%), dynamic savings table, and smart comparison. The hidden Edge audit paid a selected installment, changed penalty to 5%, verified 300.00 principal, 80.00 interest, 20.00 tax, 15.00 penalty, 85.00 savings, and confirmed the remaining installments and early-closed loan status. It reported zero browser exceptions. The web suite passed 634 tests (946 deselected); focused dispatch and reference checks passed 28 tests.
Legacy installment status value dendi is handled as paid, matching the desktop filter. Full desktop-to-web parity remains incomplete and continues in focused slices.

Bulk loan footer parity follow-up (2026-10-17): aligned actions to the desktop order (select all, early close, pay all, pay selected, close). The pay-all action now marks every unpaid installment selected and refreshes the displayed total before submitting. Hidden Edge verified multi-loan selection, pay-all writes, footer order, and zero browser exceptions.
Bulk loan footer verification follow-up (2026-10-17): the complete web-selected test suite passed after the action-order and pay-all selection changes (634 passed, 946 deselected). Focused dispatch and reference-navigation tests pass (28). Source syntax, ASCII, mojibake, and diff guards pass.

Dialog catalog manifest serving correction (2026-10-17): the extractor previously searched an empty Web_Arayuzu/src tree and wrote to web_interface/static, while the API served web/dialogs.generated.json; the file was absent, so the dialog catalog API returned an empty manifest. Corrected source and output paths, repaired the standalone extractor imports, and escaped generated JSON Unicode to handle source surrogate characters. Regenerated the runtime manifest from the 114 desktop dialog modules (276 buttons, 809 fields, 391 connections). A hidden Edge run loaded all 114 catalog cards, filtered to bulk_payment_dialog, and completed the selected, pay-all, and early-close workflows with zero browser exceptions. The dialog catalog and bulk payment flow now have functional offscreen coverage.
Dialog manifest serving regression verification (2026-10-17): the full web-selected test suite passes with the generated dialog manifest and new path contract (635 passed, 946 deselected, one Python 3.13 aifc deprecation warning). The manifest dispatch suite passes 11 tests; extractor/verifier compile, ASCII/mojibake checks, and git diff whitespace checks pass.

Customer notes full-suite verification (2026-10-17): 636 web-selected tests passed, 946 were deselected, and one dependency deprecation warning was reported. Focused dispatch/navigation tests pass (30); source syntax, ASCII, mojibake, and diff guards pass.

- Service-list row selection and mouse context menu (2026-10-17): comparison with the native QTableWidget event filter showed that the web menu builder was not connected to rows in the main service-list route. Row clicks now retain a single current row separately from bulk checkbox selection; right-click selects that row and opens the existing permission-aware menu, including the nested print choices. Hidden Edge verified clicked-row state, context-menu prevention and first action, the print submenu trigger, Shift+F10, menu focus, existing filters, and zero browser exceptions.

- Service-list current-row visual state (2026-10-17): the hidden Edge capture confirmed the clicked row fill is RGB 30, 48, 77 (#1E304D), matching the desktop dark-blue theme `finance_active_bg`; checkbox bulk selection remains a separate state.

- Service-list report export (2026-10-17): the export dialog now offers Excel, CSV, and PDF for the complete filtered result set and selected filtered rows, with the desktop report columns and date-stamped filenames. Authenticated tenant-context POST endpoints build XLSX and landscape PDF output; valid archive mode permits these read-only exports, and the export data is bounded and formula-safe. Hidden Edge verified the dialog flow, selected-row CSV and complete filtered result set, request bodies, filenames, and desktop-matched CSV headers with zero browser exceptions. Authenticated HTTP integration verifies the XLSX and PDF responses, formula-safe text cells, tenant login, cross-origin rejection, invalid archive rejection, and invalid-field rejection. Live production-account downloads remain unverified.
- Service-list direct detailed receipt (2026-10-17): the desktop quick action prints the detailed receipt for the current row; the web quick action previously printed the whole list. It now opens the detailed receipt for the current service row and prompts when no row is selected. Hidden Edge verified that the selected tracking number is sent to the receipt renderer, together with the list selection, filters, and exports, with zero browser exceptions.
- Matched sidebar visual follow-up (2026-10-17): regenerated the native and hidden-Edge dashboard shell captures at 1280x800. The 256x800 sidebar crop improved from RGB MAE 9.982 / 57.55% exact to 9.693 / 58.13%; hidden Edge audited 73 routes across both sectors with no missing route IDs, missing icons, duplicates, or browser exceptions. - Logistics manifest and report actions (2026-10-17): replaced whole-page printing with a desktop-style manifest for the clicked row or all currently filtered rows; the manifest includes customer/device, service, outbound and return carriers, date, and status. The server, Edge process tree, and temporary data were cleaned up. Ten focused reference tests, JavaScript syntax, ASCII/mojibake guards, and diff checks pass.
- External service tracking manifest (2026-10-17): extended the hidden Edge workflow audit to verify the actual desktop-mapped external warranty/service route. Managed-user create workflow runtime follow-up (2026-10-17): extended the hidden Edge user-management audit to open the actual New User dialog, enter required identity/password fields, choose the Admin role, and submit. Authenticated managed-user creation round trip (2026-10-17): the hidden Edge audit signs in against a temporary tenant server, opens the real user-management form, submits a technician with a selected desktop permission set, and reloads the managed-user list from the tenant API. The test then checks the user, password hash, personnel row, role permissions, and actor audit record in the tenant SQLite database. The seven focused create/API/browser tests pass; browser exceptions: 0. Other create error paths remain open.

Authenticated managed-user lifecycle follow-up (2026-10-17): extended the same hidden Edge tenant session through duplicate-name rejection, successful user creation, permission/role edit, bulk password reset with the explicit second confirmation, and soft deletion. The scoped browser/API suite passes 13 tests with zero browser exceptions. Broader user-management error cases and unrelated page workflows remain open.

Authenticated TV status refresh follow-up (2026-10-17): hidden Edge signs into a temporary tenant, renders its real service-status board, and observes the next automatic devices API read 4.5-8 seconds after the prior read. The test changes TV-A-001 from Tamirde to Teslim Edildi through the tenant API and confirms the status appears in the board after its next poll and in tenant SQLite. The 5000 ms timer is registered and browser exceptions remain zero. The existing timer callback check, authenticated tenant API test, and browser-to-tenant integration pass; 18 focused service-status tests pass. Matched Qt pixel comparison remains open.

Tenant-local role isolation correction (2026-10-17): `ensure_control_owner()` previously changed every non-vendor tenant account to `User` each time control-admin status was checked, erasing local Admin and Technician roles and their interface permissions. It now limits central control access through the `control_admins` registry while preserving tenant-local roles. Hidden Edge signed in as an actual tenant Technician and verified technical and automotive menu permissions from the authenticated session; SQLite retained the role and permissions, and the account had no central control access. Vendor-center, sidebar-auth, and user-management regression suites pass 60 tests with zero browser exceptions. Whole-app page and visual parity remains open.
Tenant-local Admin lifecycle verification (2026-10-17): repeated the hidden Edge create/edit/bulk-password/delete round trip as the persisted tenant `Admin` account. The authenticated user remained `Admin` with `is_control_admin=false`; tenant database state and audit actor IDs matched that local account. The 60-test vendor-center, sidebar-auth, and user-management regression suite passes with zero browser exceptions. Whole-app page and visual parity remains open.
Web regression sweep (2026-10-17): `python -m pytest tests -q -k web --tb=short` passes 736 tests with 956 deselected and one upstream Python 3.13 `aifc` deprecation warning. This broad regression result does not replace page-by-page behavior mapping, full dialog review, signal mapping, authenticated workflow coverage across schema families, or matched-size visual comparison.
E-invoice keyboard parity (2026-10-17): compared the active desktop composer shortcuts (`F2` add line, `F4` focus customer, `F12` send invoice) with Web_Arayuzu. Hidden Edge used a tenant-authenticated Admin session to dispatch all three keys against the rendered browser composer; it verified the new line, customer focus, and the send request payload with zero browser exceptions. The focused e-invoice reference/composer suite passes 16 tests.
Loaner search shortcut parity (2026-10-17): the desktop `Ctrl+F` shortcut focuses and selects the loaner search field; Web_Arayuzu now handles the same key on the loaner page, prevents browser find, and focuses/selects `genericSearch`. Hidden Edge verified focus, selection, and default prevention with a tenant Admin session and zero browser exceptions. Loaner reference/return/pagination tests pass 24 tests.
Contract search shortcut parity (2026-10-17): the desktop `Ctrl+K` shortcut focuses its contract search field, while the web global-search listener previously opened the global palette and shadowed that page action. The listener now routes `Ctrl+K` to the contracts filter when the contract page is active and keeps the global palette on other pages. Hidden Edge verified the focused/selected contract filter and that the global palette remained closed under an authenticated tenant Admin session. Loaner, contract, pagination, and global-search suites pass 36 tests.
Report-template search shortcut parity (2026-10-17): the desktop page binds `Ctrl+F` to its template search field; the web page now routes the same key to its generic search input and prevents browser find. Hidden Edge verified focus, full-text selection, and default prevention with a tenant Admin session. Loaner, contract, pagination, and global-search regression suites pass 37 tests.
Customer search shortcut parity (2026-10-17): the desktop customer pages bind `Ctrl+K` to the customer filter, while the global search palette used to capture that shortcut on every page. The global-search handler now routes `Ctrl+K` to `customerSearch` while the customer page is active and retains the palette elsewhere. Hidden Edge verified the filter receives focus and selection and that the palette stays closed for a tenant Admin. Customer, global search, loaner, contracts, report-template, and pagination suites pass 58 tests.
Contract metrics parity correction (2026-10-17): contract web table rendering had replaced the desktop's unmeasured Average SLA Response Time placeholder (`-`) with the total contract count. Removed that incorrect update. Authenticated hidden Edge loaded the real tenant contract table and verified the metric remains `-`; contract attachment create/delete still passed in the same browser run. Focused contract/attachment tests pass 15 tests.

Technical Service header parity correction (2026-10-17): the Technical Service desktop `AppHeader` exposes sync, notifications, interface editing, settings, shortcuts, theme, and window controls, but has no global quick-add action. The web shell's similarly named quick-add opened a different picker and was extra to the desktop shell; removed it from the global header while retaining dashboard-level new-transaction actions. Focused source regression and hidden Edge shell verification confirm the global header action is absent and the quick-add handler remains available to page-level workflows. Whole-shell matched screenshot parity remains open.

Desktop dashboard-shortcut header parity (2026-10-17): desktop exposes its star shortcut control at header widths of 1400 px and above and toggles the dashboard shortcut overlay. Added a width-matched web header control wired to the same dashboard overlay state. Hidden Edge verified the overlay closes and reopens from the header at 1500x900 and that the control is hidden at 1280x800. Page-by-page visual parity remains open.

Desktop assistant sidebar workflow (2026-10-17): the compact Technical Service header's robot control toggles `AssistantSidebarDialog`; the web header previously navigated to a separate assistant reference page. Added a matching 450x700 chat panel with header toggle, close/Escape behavior, scrollable messages, Enter submission, and the desktop built-in finance, stock, and appointment routes. Hidden Edge opened the panel from the actual web shell, sent `finans ozeti`, verified the finance route and assistant reply, closed with Escape, and reported zero browser exceptions. Unmatched questions use the authenticated web AI query route; voice-provider selection remains an open parity gap.

Desktop assistant drag and voice parity (2026-10-17): added pointer dragging for the web assistant panel and tenant-gated speech replies. The panel now requests WAV audio from the authenticated local EMA TTS route, then falls back to Turkish browser speech when the provider is unavailable. Hidden Edge verified a 120x40 panel move, local audio playback when enabled, no speech request when disabled, and zero browser exceptions. The desktop assistant sidebar also uses the local EMA provider via its auto engine; the voice settings page now exposes the same single local model.

Desktop assistant connected query follow-up (2026-10-17): unmatched assistant-panel questions post to authenticated `/api/desktop/assistant/query`; replies use `/api/desktop/assistant/speech`, which synthesizes temporary WAV files through the same local EMA helper as the desktop sidebar and removes them after sending. Hidden Edge verified the query and audio requests, success and error display, voice-off behavior, and zero browser exceptions using stubs. `tests/test_web_ai_assistant.py` covers login enforcement, bounded speech text, WAV response, temporary-file cleanup, and active-tenant query selection. The installed isolated EMA runtime also synthesized a real WAV successfully; its worker was stopped after verification.


Desktop voice settings rendering and preview parity (2026-10-17): a later generic settings renderer had shadowed the dedicated voice settings page and exposed obsolete Edge/system provider choices. Restored the dedicated page, aligned its single local EMA model with the desktop controls, and wired the speech preview button to the authenticated local EMA WAV route using the current assistant name and greeting prefix. Hidden Edge verified the voice settings page button, request payload, playback, button re-enabling, the scenario-settings shortcut routing to the web voice command editor, and the browser microphone picker persisting its selected device locally while the test stops every capture track and closes its audio context after sampling, with zero browser exceptions using stubbed media. `tests/test_web_voice_reference.py` covers the renderer dispatch and local speech contract.


Voice scheduled-alert parity (2026-10-17): the web voice settings previously exposed three of the desktop's four reminders, and bootstrap omitted disabled alerts. The settings now show the service-delivery alert at its desktop default of 17:30, preserve its enabled state, and include all tenant alert rows in bootstrap; browser dispatch now skips disabled alerts. The authenticated tenant test verified a disabled service-delivery record survives bootstrap, and hidden Edge verified all four cards and the unchecked state.


Scheduled-alert runtime parity (2026-10-17): web reminders now honor the desktop scheduled-alert and voice-alert feature flags. They always keep the in-app toast, suppress browser notification and EMA speech during quiet hours for noncritical alerts, and allow critical alerts through; speech uses the authenticated local EMA WAV route. Hidden Edge verified quiet-hour suppression, normal speech and notification delivery, critical bypass, disabled voice-alert behavior, and full scheduler shutdown gates using deterministic local times and stubbed audio. Each fired alert now persists `last_triggered` to the tenant table, and the authenticated API test verified that bootstrap returns the saved timestamp to prevent repeats after reload.

Voice scheduled-alert editing parity (2026-10-17): settings now expose editable HH:MM controls beside each of the four reminder toggles. Saves use the authenticated tenant table API and keep the previous UI value on failure; API validation rejects invalid 24-hour times and enabled flags. Hidden Edge verified editing 17:30 to 17:45, enabling the alert, and zero browser exceptions. Focused API/UI tests pass (12 total); broader migration remains open.

Service destructive-dialog offscreen verification (2026-10-17): a file-URL Edge audit exercises standalone service deletion and photo deletion with both cancel and submit paths, plus the technician-panel close prompt. Stubbed tenant APIs confirmed that cancel sends no destructive write and submit sends the expected tenant API payloads; no native browser confirm was invoked and there were zero browser exceptions. This closes the earlier verification gap for these dialogs; authenticated live-tenant behavior remains separate and unverified.

Assistant voice-provider selection gap closure (2026-10-17): the earlier sidebar entry predates the dedicated voice-settings and speech-route follow-ups. Desktop header height refinement (2026-10-17): the web topbar had an 82px desktop height while native AppHeader is fixed at 70px. The desktop-width topbar now uses 70px height and the native 8px/6px horizontal insets; hidden Edge asserts the resulting 70px bounds at 1500x900. The prior 1280x800 shell pixel score predates this adjustment and needs a fresh paired capture before reporting an updated visual score; full-shell parity remains open.

Desktop header brand geometry refinement (2026-10-17): at 1280px the web header now follows the native order (28px logo, PRO marker, separator, 56x24px Back control, separator, AYEC Pro name, flexible route title) and preserves the same 70px frame. Hidden Edge asserted logo/back dimensions, brand labels and order, header shortcuts at desktop/compact widths, and the assistant-panel path with zero exceptions. The paired shell pixel score is not refreshed because the file-URL capture does not reproduce the authenticated tenant theme; full-shell visual parity remains open.

Compact header regression after brand alignment (2026-10-17): the new PRO marker and dividers hide with the desktop logo and Back button under the 760px breakpoint. Hidden Edge at 390x844 verifies the menu button remains available and the topbar has no horizontal overflow; browser exceptions remain zero.

Desktop header action sizing refinement (2026-10-17): web sync, settings, and notification controls now measure 26x26px, while the interface-edit button measures 26px high, matching the native AppHeader fixed control sizes. The hidden Edge header audit asserts those dimensions together with the brand order and compact 390px layout; zero runtime exceptions.

Customer address-to-map workflow verification (2026-10-17): hidden Edge now fills the customer address, invokes the authenticated geocoder, selects a returned result, stores its coordinates, then sends those coordinates through reverse geocoding and fills the normalized address fields. Fixture providers returned deterministic Nominatim/Photon-shaped data; the browser reported zero exceptions. tests/test_web_field_service_personnel.py remains the focused backend coverage; live external provider and TomTom tile behavior remain unverified.

No live provider request is used; browser exceptions remain zero.

Desktop header source reconciliation (2026-10-17): inspection of the shared native AppHeader presentation showed that its current standard order is Back (80x32px), the 10px accent dot, AYEC Pro (72px label), then the route title; the PRO badge and separators are hidden. The web header and hidden Edge geometry assertion now follow that source contract. This supersedes the earlier brand-geometry note above, which reflected a web-only arrangement rather than the current native layout.

Finance transaction Customer 360 action parity (2026-10-17): the web transaction detail footer now shows the native Customer 360 action when a customer ID is linked and opens that customer's 360 view. Finance transaction tracking copy parity (2026-10-17): transaction details now resolve tracking numbers from the explicit tracking/reference fields or the native `Ref:` description pattern and provide the inline copy control when one exists. Finance transaction linked-service line parity (2026-10-17): when a transaction lacks embedded line items, the web detail table now derives work lines, labor, used parts, and cargo from the linked service record, matching the desktop lookup priority for device service data. Finance receipt content layout alignment (2026-10-17): the isolated web print document now emits the same seven plain text rows and line-break structure as the desktop QTextDocument receipt, instead of adding web-only headings, borders, and paragraph spacing. Finance detail service description and audit metadata parity (2026-10-17): when linked service work lines exist, the detail note now includes them separately from the transaction note; the operator and timestamp prefer the same native entry fields before their fallbacks. PC Builder desktop preset metrics (2026-10-17): at widths above 1200px, the preset bar now uses the native 10px corner radius and a 12px label. PC Builder action-bar reconciliation (2026-10-17): moved the Customer 360 action from the customer-selection row into the native bottom action bar, between PDF and stock actions. The matched 1300x850 whole-window RGB MAE improved from 12.766 to 12.349; customer-bar crop MAE improved from 13.067 to 11.863. PC Builder selected-customer header parity (2026-10-17): selecting a customer now updates the offer header with the customer name, matching the native `CustomerSearchBar` callback; clearing selection removes that suffix, and search filtering preserves it while the selected customer remains visible. PC Builder customer-note content parity (2026-10-17): the web now formats Customer 360 quote notes and saved offer audit details like the native workflow, including bullet/multiplication markers, grouped two-decimal TRY amounts, and `dd.MM.yyyy HH:mm` timestamps. The focused regression and offscreen audit pass; broader PC Builder persistence and page parity remain open.
PC Builder preset-content parity (2026-10-17): corrected the Content Creator and Workstation preset notes to preserve the desktop ampersands in the generated customer-facing text. PC Builder custom-component dialog parity (2026-10-17): aligned the web dialog with the native three fields: component name, stock category, and a specification label defaulting to `Standart` (empty optional labels fall back to `Tip`). New rows start with the native `Standart` option. Hidden Edge submits a custom Optical Drive row and verifies the dialog defaults, fields, row label/options, and closure; the focused regression and browser audit pass with zero exceptions.
PC Builder brand filter parity (2026-10-17): web brand choices now include the same category-specific common brands and infer additional brands from product names like the desktop; matching uses the native alias rules (including G.Skill/GSkill) and searches product name, code, and description rather than trusting a separate brand field. Hidden Edge confirms RAM brand choices, G.Skill alias filtering, and exclusion of a brand-only `Audit` field. Focused regression, browser audit, and source guards pass.
PC Builder filter-selection consistency (2026-10-17): selecting a stock product now updates the visible unit price and stock badge. Changing brand/spec filters preserves a selected product only while it still matches; an excluded product clears its selection, price, and stock, matching the native row reset behavior. Hidden Edge verifies product price/stock display, G.Skill filtering, and brand/spec reset paths; focused regression and browser audit pass.
PC Builder stock deduction partial-success parity (2026-10-17): insufficient components no longer cancel valid stock deductions. Web groups duplicate part IDs and sends only in-stock quantities through the tenant-atomic deduction endpoint, then reports the successful rows and each insufficient component; refreshed stock labels and prices follow the saved inventory. Hidden Edge selected two valid parts and one over-stock RAM row, verified only the valid parts were posted, their stock fell, RAM stayed unchanged, and both success/error feedback appeared. Focused regression and browser audit pass with zero exceptions.

Finance transaction receipt field parity (2026-10-17): aligned the web print receipt with native TransactionDetailsDialog.print_receipt field selection. TRY totals use amount_try, foreign currency totals use amount, and date, type, and customer use the native date, type_label, and customer_name fields. Hidden Edge used conflicting fallback values and verified the exact TRY and USD amounts, field selection, escaping, and plain receipt layout; focused finance-detail regression and ASCII/mojibake guards pass. ### PC Builder quantity range parity

Aligned component quantities with the desktop stepper range of 0 through 99. The web input accepts zero, clamps typed or programmatic values above 99, and keeps zero-quantity rows unfilled. The focused Python regression passed (8 tests), and the offscreen Edge audit verified zero, upper-bound clamping, restoration of the original quantity, and zero browser exceptions.

### Security confirmation and automatic login parity

Mapped the security confirmation dialog to a tenant authenticated action-password check with failed-attempt rate limiting. The security settings save now asks for the current password when automatic login changes, and saves only after verification. Fixed the security save handler to target the rendered settings content container. Focused API tests pass (2); hidden Edge verified wrong-password rejection, valid-password acceptance, the manifest route, automatic-login reauthentication before save, and zero browser exceptions. Native OS automatic sign-in remains limited by browser/runtime capabilities.

- Security confirmation follow-up: the automatic-login endpoint now requires the current password in the same request that changes the preference, checks it server-side, and rate-limits failed attempts. This prevents bypassing the dialog by calling the API directly. The focused API test verifies wrong-password rejection leaves the preference unchanged; hidden Edge verifies the password accompanies the save. The security settings page still records a desktop startup preference; browser launch behavior is runtime-limited.

- Automatic-login verification also confirms the preference update stays scoped to the signed-in account; a second tenant user remains unchanged after both the successful and rejected update cases.

### Sales cart context quantity and price parity

Service cart rows now support decimal quantities and comma-decimal entry in the context menu, matching the desktop double input. Service price edits update both stored price fields; stock quantities match the desktop integer stepper range. Hidden Edge exercised the actual row context menu for service quantity, price, description, copy, remove, and stock quantity changes; the fixture was restored before subsequent sales checks. Focused Sales Hub tests pass (6), and the existing offscreen Sales Hub audit passes.

- Sales commit payload follow-up: the hidden Edge check also confirmed a 1.5-unit service at 150.75 TRY reaches the authenticated sales commit request without rounding, alongside the selected transaction date.
- Sales workflow offscreen refresh (2026-10-18): reran `sales_bulk_f2_offscreen.cjs` through an isolated temporary server and changed its wrapper to run browser profiles and intermediate captures under the temporary directory, then preserve the final screenshots in a dated folder. The audit now captures and fails on browser exceptions; the rerun reported none. It passed F5/F8/F10/F12 actions, stock and service line composition, quantity and price edits, descriptions, copy/remove context actions, date propagation, and the selected transaction payload. Captures and JSON result: `output/playwright/sales-cart-context-refresh-2026-10-17T22-10-34-840Z/`.

The service status dialog and repair filter now substitute Serviste for Tamirde in the automotive sector, matching the desktop status menu. The focused service-list regression passes (10 tests); hidden Edge verified automotive and technical dialog choices and filter labels with zero browser exceptions. ASCII/mojibake guards pass.

Added the missing browser-global stub to that test fixture; the dashboard parity suite passes (10 tests). The service status focused regressions and hidden Edge audit also pass.

### PC Builder inline quantity and price controls

Component rows now use native-style minus/input/plus controls for quantity and unit price. Quantity keeps the native 0-99 range; price uses the native 0-999999 range and two decimal places, with the TRY suffix. Hidden Edge verified both controls increment and decrement, and that row totals update; the full PC Builder offscreen audit completed with zero browser exceptions. Focused PC Builder regressions pass (9 tests).

- Full web regression verification (2026-10-17): after updating the dashboard test VM browser global and making the contracts SLA assertion independent of asynchronous page startup, all 716 	est_web_*.py tests pass. - PC Builder decimal-entry follow-up: unit prices use text decimal input so the native dot-formatted value remains visible under Turkish browser locale; comma decimal input is normalized and clamped to the native range. Hidden Edge verified 100,75 normalization, 101.75 increment, 100.75 decrement, maximum clamping, and updated total.

- Service receipt visual follow-up (2026-10-17): removed the web-only outlines and horizontal inset around fault/repair note values to match the native receipt's unboxed text. The print header content now sits 2px lower and the title phrase uses a horizontal glyph scale to match the native font metrics without changing document flow. The right info labels now align one pixel closer to the native columns, while their value columns retain their previous alignment. Hidden Edge confirmed all receipt content and print settings, one-page output, and zero browser exceptions. The refreshed hidden Edge/Qt paired capture improved PDF raster MAE from 11.017 to 10.624, exact pixels from 86.15% to 86.28%, and p95 channel delta from 80 to 73 at 893 x 630; a 28% company-header column trial based on the native PDF source worsened MAE to 12.765, a 17px company-title trial worsened MAE to 10.866 with p95 77, and a 17.73px part-row line-height trial worsened MAE to 10.703 with p95 75; all were reverted. A clean baseline recheck measured MAE 10.620, exact pixels 86.29%, and p95 delta 73, with one-page output and zero browser exceptions; visual parity remains open. Recheck evidence: `output/playwright/service-receipt-recheck-20261017/service-detailed-receipt-web.pdf` and `output/playwright/service-receipt-recheck-20261017/service-detailed-receipt-headless.png`. Evidence: `output/playwright/service-receipt-parity-20261017/service-detailed-receipt-desktop.pdf`, `output/playwright/service-receipt-parity-20261017/service-detailed-receipt-web.pdf`, and `output/playwright/service-receipt-parity-20261017/service-detailed-receipt-headless.png`.

- Offer management filtering parity (2026-10-17): added status and currency filters to the offer list, matching the desktop pending/accepted/rejected and TRY/USD/EUR choices. The six-row page controls, result count, selected-offer preview, and CSV export follow the current filters and page. Hidden Edge verified accepted EUR, empty accepted USD, pending USD, and navigation across 7 filtered results; focused offer filter tests pass (4).

- Offer report aggregation parity (2026-10-17): aligned status buckets and TRY totals with the desktop report service, bounded the trend to the last 12 calendar months, grouped customer totals by company/customer and ranked customers/personnel by offer count before amount (top 20).
